Persecutory Delusions: The false belief that one is being harmed or monitored by other people or organizations.\n  - Referential Delusions: The conviction that ordinary events, remarks, or behaviors have specific relevance to oneself.\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Erotomanic Delusions: The false belief that another person is in love with or has affection for them.\n  - Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Indifference to Achievement: Insensitivity or indifference to personal achievements or success.\n  - Chronic Fatigue: Fatigue that persists for a long period even after adequate rest.\n  - Physical Fatigue: A state of physical exhaustion where the body feels heavy and easily tires even from minor activities.\n  - Mental Fatigue: A state where fatigue accumulates rapidly during the process of thinking or concentrating.\n  - Loss of Energy: Difficulty performing daily activities due to a decline in energy and vitality.\n  - Excessive or Inappropriate Guilt: Feeling excessive responsibility for trivial matters or daily events and believing one deserves punishment.\n  - Delusional Guilt: Guilt reaching a delusional level, disconnected from reality, involving feeling responsible for unrelated unfortunate events.\n  - Recurrent Thoughts of Death: Passive wish to die (e.g., 'I wish I wouldn't wake up') without active intent to kill oneself.\n  - Suicidal Ideation: Recurring thoughts of 'I want to die' or 'I want to kill myself' without a specific plan.\n  - Suicidal Impulse: Strong, sudden urges to jump from a height or run into traffic without a concrete plan.\n  - Specific Plan for Suicide: Conceiving specific scenarios or methods regarding when, where, and how to die.\n  - Suicide Attempt: Actual actions taken to end one's life or preparatory behaviors like hoarding pills or writing a will.\n  Duration: 7 months\n▸ Critical Diagnostic Criteria\n  - Additional: delusions_and_hallucinations occur exclusively during the manic episode\n\nRules:\n- If the patient can specifically answer based on the profile, set matched=true, list related\n  sections/items in matched_sections, and write a short English strategy in answer_strategy\n  on what tone and content the patient should reply with.\n- If the question is not in the profile or can only be inferred, set matched=false and instruct\n  in answer_strategy to respond briefly (e.g., \"I'm not sure\", \"I haven't thought about it\").\n- If patient_turn_index is 1 (patient's first reply), ONLY focus the strategy on the chief complaint\n  (do NOT reveal other symptoms).\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"matched\": true|false, \"matched_sections\": [\"...\"], \"answer_strategy\": \"...\"}\n"

Increased Energy: Overflowing physical and mental energy, leading to an inability to stay still and a desire to keep moving.\n  - Hyperactivity: Doing far more work than usual or starting multiple activities simultaneously.\n  - Psychomotor Agitation: Inability to tolerate resting or stopping, driven by a constant need to do something.\n  - Responsiveness to Extraneous Stimuli: Attention is immediately drawn to trivial and unimportant stimuli nearby (e.g., noise, movement).\n  - Frequent Task Switching: Unable to finish one task and constantly shifting attention to new things, starting multiple tasks simultaneously.\n  - Inability to Sustain Attention: Unable to focus on any activity for long, with attention constantly being scattered.\n  Duration: 4 months\n▸ delusions_and_hallucinations\n  - Persecutory Delusions: The false belief that one is being harmed or monitored by other people or organizations.\n  - Referential Delusions: The conviction that ordinary events, remarks, or behaviors have specific relevance to oneself.\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Erotomanic Delusions: The false belief that another person is in love with or has affection for them.\n  - Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Auditory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of hearing voices or sounds when there is no actual sound.\n  - Visual Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of seeing people or objects that do not exist.\n  - Tactile Hallucinations: Experiencing the sensation of being touched or movement on the skin when there is no actual stimulus.\n  - Emptiness: A feeling of inner emptiness or a state of feeling no emotion.\n  - Emotional Numbness: A state where emotions feel dulled or paralyzed.\n  - Loss of Interest: Reduced interest in previously enjoyed hobbies or leisure activities.\n  - Social Withdrawal: Reduced interest or effort in social interactions and maintaining relationships.\n  - Loss of Motivation: Lack of motivation to initiate or complete work or tasks.\n  - Anhedonia: Inability to experience positive emotions such as happiness, satisfaction, love, or pleasure.\n  - Indifference to Achievement: Insensitivity or indifference to personal achievements or success.\n  - Chronic Fatigue: Fatigue that persists for a long period even after adequate rest.\n  - Physical Fatigue: A state of physical exhaustion where the body feels heavy and easily tires even from minor activities.\n  - Mental Fatigue: A state where fatigue accumulates rapidly during the process of thinking or concentrating.\n  - Loss of Energy: Difficulty performing daily activities due to a decline in energy and vitality.\n  - Feelings of Worthlessness: The feeling that one is useless and has no value.\n  - Ruminative Guilt: A thought pattern of obsessively replaying past minor mistakes or flaws and constantly reproaching oneself.\n  - Excessive or Inappropriate Guilt: Feeling excessive responsibility for trivial matters or daily events and believing one deserves punishment.\n  - Delusional Guilt: Guilt reaching a delusional level, disconnected from reality, involving feeling responsible for unrelated unfortunate events.\n  - Recurrent Thoughts of Death: Passive wish to die (e.g., 'I wish I wouldn't wake up') without active intent to kill oneself.\n  - Suicidal Ideation: Recurring thoughts of 'I want to die' or 'I want to kill myself' without a specific plan.\n  - Suicidal Impulse: Strong, sudden urges to jump from a height or run into traffic without a concrete plan.\n  - Specific Plan for Suicide: Conceiving specific scenarios or methods regarding when, where, and how to die.\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Erotomanic Delusions: The false belief that another person is in love with or has affection for them.\n  - Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Auditory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of hearing voices or sounds when there is no actual sound.\n  - Persecutory Delusions: The false belief that one is being harmed or monitored by other people or organizations.\n  - Referential Delusions: The conviction that ordinary events, remarks, or behaviors have specific relevance to oneself.\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Erotomanic Delusions: The false belief that another person is in love with or has affection for them.\n  - Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Auditory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of hearing voices or sounds when there is no actual sound.\n  - Visual Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of seeing people or objects that do not exist.\n  - Tactile Hallucinations: Experiencing the sensation of being touched or movement on the skin when there is no actual stimulus.\n  - Social Withdrawal: Reduced interest or effort in social interactions and maintaining relationships.\n  - Loss of Motivation: Lack of motivation to initiate or complete work or tasks.\n  - Anhedonia: Inability to experience positive emotions such as happiness, satisfaction, love, or pleasure.\n  - Indifference to Achievement: Insensitivity or indifference to personal achievements or success.\n  - Chronic Fatigue: Fatigue that persists for a long period even after adequate rest.\n  - Physical Fatigue: A state of physical exhaustion where the body feels heavy and easily tires even from minor activities.\n  - Mental Fatigue: A state where fatigue accumulates rapidly during the process of thinking or concentrating.\n  - Loss of Energy: Difficulty performing daily activities due to a decline in energy and vitality.\n  - Excessive or Inappropriate Guilt: Feeling excessive responsibility for trivial matters or daily events and believing one deserves punishment.\n  - Delusional Guilt: Guilt reaching a delusional level, disconnected from reality, involving feeling responsible for unrelated unfortunate events.\n  - Recurrent Thoughts of Death: Passive wish to die (e.g., 'I wish I wouldn't wake up') without active intent to kill oneself.\n  - Suicidal Ideation: Recurring thoughts of 'I want to die' or 'I want to kill myself' without a specific plan.\n  - Elevated Mood: A mood that is abnormally high, euphoric, and full of unmotivated happiness and vitality.\n  - Irritable Mood: A state where one gets angry or aggressive even at minor stimuli.\n  - Mood Lability: Rapid shifts in mood, alternating unpredictably between euphoria and irritability.\n  - Increased Energy: Overflowing physical and mental energy, leading to an inability to stay still and a desire to keep moving.\n  - Hyperactivity: Doing far more work than usual or starting multiple activities simultaneously.\n  - Psychomotor Agitation: Inability to tolerate resting or stopping, driven by a constant need to do something.\n  - Responsiveness to Extraneous Stimuli: Attention is immediately drawn to trivial and unimportant stimuli nearby (e.g., noise, movement).\n  - Frequent Task Switching: Unable to finish one task and constantly shifting attention to new things, starting multiple tasks simultaneously.\n  - Inability to Sustain Attention: Unable to focus on any activity for long, with attention constantly being scattered.\n  Duration: 4 months\n▸ delusions_and_hallucinations\n  - Persecutory Delusions: The false belief that one is being harmed or monitored by other people or organizations.\n  - Referential Delusions: The conviction that ordinary events, remarks, or behaviors have specific relevance to oneself.\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Erotomanic Delusions: The false belief that another person is in love with or has affection for them.\n  - Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Auditory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of hearing voices or sounds when there is no actual sound.\n  - Visual Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of seeing people or objects that do not exist.\n  - Tactile Hallucinations: Experiencing the sensation of being touched or movement on the skin when there is no actual stimulus.\n  - Olfactory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of smelling odors that do not exist.\n  - Gustatory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of tasting flavors that are not actually present.\n▸ depressive_episode\n  - Depressed Mood: A persistently sunken mood or feeling of depression.\n  - Tearfulness: A state of frequent crying or inability to hold back tears.\n  - Sadness: A state where a sad or gloomy mood is maintained continuously.\n  - Emptiness: A feeling of inner emptiness or a state of feeling no emotion.\n  - Emotional Numbness: A state where emotions feel dulled or paralyzed.\n  - Loss of Interest: Reduced interest in previously enjoyed hobbies or leisure activities.\n  - Social Withdrawal: Reduced interest or effort in social interactions and maintaining relationships.\n  - Loss of Motivation: Lack of motivation to initiate or complete work or tasks.\n  - Anhedonia: Inability to experience positive emotions such as happiness, satisfaction, love, or pleasure.\n  - Indifference to Achievement: Insensitivity or indifference to personal achievements or success.\n  - Chronic Fatigue: Fatigue that persists for a long period even after adequate rest.\n  - Physical Fatigue: A state of physical exhaustion where the body feels heavy and easily tires even from minor activities.\n  - Mental Fatigue: A state where fatigue accumulates rapidly during the process of thinking or concentrating.\n  - Loss of Energy: Difficulty performing daily activities due to a decline in energy and vitality.\n  - Feelings of Worthlessness: The feeling that one is useless and has no value.\n  - Ruminative Guilt: A thought pattern of obsessively replaying past minor mistakes or flaws and constantly reproaching oneself.\n  - Excessive or Inappropriate Guilt: Feeling excessive responsibility for trivial matters or daily events and believing one deserves punishment.\n  - Delusional Guilt: Guilt reaching a delusional level, disconnected from reality, involving feeling responsible for unrelated unfortunate events.\n  - Recurrent Thoughts of Death: Passive wish to die (e.g., 'I wish I wouldn't wake up') without active intent to kill oneself.\n  - Suicidal Ideation: Recurring thoughts of 'I want to die' or 'I want to kill myself' without a specific plan.\n  - Suicidal Impulse: Strong, sudden urges to jump from a height or run into traffic without a concrete plan.\n  - Specific Plan for Suicide: Conceiving specific scenarios or methods regarding when, where, and how to die.\n  - Suicide Attempt: Actual actions taken to end one's life or preparatory behaviors like hoarding pills or writing a will.\n  Duration: 7 months\n▸ Critical Diagnostic Criteria\n  - Additional: delusions_and_hallucinations occur exclusively during the manic episode\n\nRules:\n- If the patient can specifically answer based on the profile, set matched=true, list related\n  sections/items in matched_sections, and write a short English strategy in answer_strategy\n  on what tone and content the patient should reply with.\n- If the question is not in the profile or can only be inferred, set matched=false and instruct\n  in answer_strategy to respond briefly (e.g., \"I'm not sure\", \"I haven't thought about it\").\n- If patient_turn_index is 1 (patient's first reply), ONLY focus the strategy on the chief complaint\n  (do NOT reveal other symptoms).\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"matched\": true|false, \"matched_sections\": [\"...\"], \"answer_strategy\": \"...\"}\n"

[

    "role": "system",
    "content": "/no_think\nYou are an assistant analyst connecting the [Symptom Profile] with the doctor's question.\nDetermine which part of the profile (chief complaint, associated symptoms, social history, etc.)\nthe doctor's **last question** is related to.\n\n[Symptom Profile]\n▸ manic_episode\n  - Elevated Mood: A mood that is abnormally high, euphoric, and full of unmotivated happiness and vitality.\n  - Irritable Mood: A state where one gets angry or aggressive even at minor stimuli.\n  - Mood Lability: Rapid shifts in mood, alternating unpredictably between euphoria and irritability.\n  - Increased Energy: Overflowing physical and mental energy, leading to an inability to stay still and a desire to keep moving.\n  - Hyperactivity: Doing far more work than usual or starting multiple activities simultaneously.\n  - Psychomotor Agitation: Inability to tolerate resting or stopping, driven by a constant need to do something.\n  - Responsiveness to Extraneous Stimuli: Attention is immediately drawn to trivial and unimportant stimuli nearby (e.g., noise, movement).\n  - Frequent Task Switching: Unable to finish one task and constantly shifting attention to new things, starting multiple tasks simultaneously.\n  - Inability to Sustain Attention: Unable to focus on any activity for long, with attention constantly being scattered.\n  Duration: 4 months\n▸ delusions_and_hallucinations\n  - Persecutory Delusions: The false belief that one is being harmed or monitored by other people or organizations.\n  - Referential Delusions: The conviction that ordinary events, remarks, or behaviors have specific relevance to oneself.\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Erotomanic Delusions: The false belief that another person is in love with or has affection for them.\n  - Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Auditory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of hearing voices or sounds when there is no actual sound.\n  - Visual Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of seeing people or objects that do not exist.\n  - Tactile Hallucinations: Experiencing the sensation of being touched or movement on the skin when there is no actual stimulus.\n  - Olfactory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of smelling odors that do not exist.\n  - Gustatory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of tasting flavors that are not actually present.\n▸ depressive_episode\n  - Depressed Mood: A persistently sunken mood or feeling of depression.\n  - Tearfulness: A state of frequent crying or inability to hold back tears.\n  - Sadness: A state where a sad or gloomy mood is maintained continuously.\n  - Emptiness: A feeling of inner emptiness or a state of feeling no emotion.\n  - Emotional Numbness: A state where emotions feel dulled or paralyzed.\n  - Loss of Interest: Reduced interest in previously enjoyed hobbies or leisure activities.\n  - Social Withdrawal: Reduced interest or effort in social interactions and maintaining relationships.\n  - Loss of Motivation: Lack of motivation to initiate or complete work or tasks.\n  - Anhedonia: Inability to experience positive emotions such as happiness, satisfaction, love, or pleasure.\n  - Indifference to Achievement: Insensitivity or indifference to personal achievements or success.\n  - Chronic Fatigue: Fatigue that persists for a long period even after adequate rest.\n  - Physical Fatigue: A state of physical exhaustion where the body feels heavy and easily tires even from minor activities.\n  - Mental Fatigue: A state where fatigue accumulates rapidly during the process of thinking or concentrating.\n  - Loss of Energy: Difficulty performing daily activities due to a decline in energy and vitality.\n  - Feelings of Worthlessness: The feeling that one is useless and has no value.\n  - Ruminative Guilt: A thought pattern of obsessively replaying past minor mistakes or flaws and constantly reproaching oneself.\n  - Excessive or Inappropriate Guilt: Feeling excessive responsibility for trivial matters or daily events and believing one deserves punishment.\n  - Delusional Guilt: Guilt reaching a delusional level, disconnected from reality, involving feeling responsible for unrelated unfortunate events.\n  - Recurrent Thoughts of Death: Passive wish to die (e.g., 'I wish I wouldn't wake up') without active intent to kill oneself.\n  - Suicidal Ideation: Recurring thoughts of 'I want to die' or 'I want to kill myself' without a specific plan.\n  - Suicidal Impulse: Strong, sudden urges to jump from a height or run into traffic without a concrete plan.\n  - Specific Plan for Suicide: Conceiving specific scenarios or methods regarding when, where, and how to die.\n  - Suicide Attempt: Actual actions taken to end one's life or preparatory behaviors like hoarding pills or writing a will.\n  Duration: 7 months\n▸ Critical Diagnostic Criteria\n  - Additional: delusions_and_hallucinations occur exclusively during the manic episode\n\nRules:\n- If the patient can specifically answer based on the profile, set matched=true, list related\n  sections/items in matched_sections, and write a short English strategy in answer_strategy\n  on what tone and content the patient should reply with.\n- If the question is not in the profile or can only be inferred, set matched=false and instruct\n  in answer_strategy to respond briefly (e.g., \"I'm not sure\", \"I haven't thought about it\").\n- If patient_turn_index is 1 (patient's first reply), ONLY focus the strategy on the chief complaint\n  (do NOT reveal other symptoms).\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"matched\": true|false, \"matched_sections\": [\"...\"], \"answer_strategy\": \"...\"}\n"

Suicidal Impulse: Strong, sudden urges to jump from a height or run into traffic without a concrete plan.\n  - Specific Plan for Suicide: Conceiving specific scenarios or methods regarding when, where, and how to die.\n  - Suicide Attempt: Actual actions taken to end one's life or preparatory behaviors like hoarding pills or writing a will.\n  Duration: 7 months\n▸ Critical Diagnostic Criteria\n  - Additional: delusions_and_hallucinations occur exclusively during the manic episode\n\n[Behavioral Guidelines]\n- In the very first turn, only talk about your chief complaint. Do NOT reveal any other symptoms.\n- Chief complaint for the first turn: Elevated_Irritable_Mood\n- Answer only what the doctor asks. Do not list your symptoms unprompted.\n- Speak naturally and briefly, as a real patient would (2–3 sentences max).\n- Do not mention symptoms that the doctor has not asked about.\n- If asked about a symptom not in your profile, say you are not sure.\n- Respond in English.\n\n\n[This Turn: Profile Alignment Result — Follow Strictly]\n(Matched with profile)\nRelated sections: Critical Diagnostic Criteria -> Additional: delusions_and_hallucinations occur exclusively during the manic episode, delusions_and_hallucinations, manic_episode\nAnswer strategy: Confirm that this is correct.     "content": "Below is the full consultation dialogue so far. Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see us today?\n\nPatient: I'm coming in today because I've been feeling extremely depressed and sad for the past seven months. I just feel completely drained of all my energy, and it's been really hard to even get through the day.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of elevated mood, irritability, or increased energy over the past seven months, even if they were brief?\n\nPatient: Yes, before this depression started, I had a period of about four months where my mood was incredibly high and I had endless energy. During that time, I was also extremely irritable and would snap at people over the smallest things.\n\nDoctor: During your current depressive episode or the period of high mood and irritability, have you experienced any unusual perceptions, such as hearing voices or seeing things that others couldn't, or held any beliefs that seemed real to you but might not be based in reality?\n\nPatient: Yes, during that high-energy period, I heard voices and had some really intense, strange beliefs that felt completely real at the time. However, those experiences only happened during those four months when my mood was so high and irritable. Since I've been depressed, I haven't experienced anything like that at all.\n\nDoctor: Can you clarify if the voices and strange beliefs you experienced during the high-energy period occurred only when your mood was at its peak, or were they present throughout the entire four-month period, even when your mood might have been less intense?\n\nPatient: They were present off and on throughout that entire four-month high-energy period, not just at the very peak. But they definitely only happened during those four months and never at any other time, especially not since I've been depressed.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any periods of at least two weeks where you experienced the strange beliefs or heard voices without also having a high or irritable mood during the entire four-month period?\n\nPatient: No, I never experienced those things on their own. The voices and strange beliefs only happened when my mood was elevated and irritable. They never occurred during any period when my mood was normal.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about your current depressive episode, such as how it started and if you've had any periods of significant improvement or worsening of symptoms over the past seven months?\n\nPatient: It started very gradually, like a heavy fog slowly creeping in, and it has been a constant weight with no real improvement over these past seven months. I just feel this deep emptiness and have absolutely no motivation to do anything. I'm also dealing with a constant, heavy fatigue that never seems to lift, no matter how much I rest.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any periods of at least two weeks where you experienced the strange beliefs or heard voices without also having a high or irritable mood at any point in your life, not just limited to the four-month period you mentioned earlier?\n\nPatient: No, I have never experienced those strange beliefs or voices on their own at any point in my life. They only ever happened during the periods when I was feeling extremely high, energetic, or irritable. Outside of those times, I've never had anything like that happen.\n\nDoctor: So, just to confirm, you've only experienced psychotic symptoms, such as hearing voices or having strange beliefs, when you were also experiencing episodes of elevated mood, irritability, or increased energy, and never at any other time in your life, is that correct?"

Elevated Mood: A mood that is abnormally high, euphoric, and full of unmotivated happiness and vitality.\n  - Irritable Mood: A state where one gets angry or aggressive even at minor stimuli.\n  - Mood Lability: Rapid shifts in mood, alternating unpredictably between euphoria and irritability.\n  - Increased Energy: Overflowing physical and mental energy, leading to an inability to stay still and a desire to keep moving.\n  - Hyperactivity: Doing far more work than usual or starting multiple activities simultaneously.\n  - Psychomotor Agitation: Inability to tolerate resting or stopping, driven by a constant need to do something.\n  - Responsiveness to Extraneous Stimuli: Attention is immediately drawn to trivial and unimportant stimuli nearby (e.g., noise, movement).\n  - Frequent Task Switching: Unable to finish one task and constantly shifting attention to new things, starting multiple tasks simultaneously.\n  - Inability to Sustain Attention: Unable to focus on any activity for long, with attention constantly being scattered.\n  Duration: 4 months\n▸ delusions_and_hallucinations\n  - Persecutory Delusions: The false belief that one is being harmed or monitored by other people or organizations.\n  - Referential Delusions: The conviction that ordinary events, remarks, or behaviors have specific relevance to oneself.\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Erotomanic Delusions: The false belief that another person is in love with or has affection for them.\n  - Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Auditory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of hearing voices or sounds when there is no actual sound.\n  - Visual Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of seeing people or objects that do not exist.\n  - Tactile Hallucinations: Experiencing the sensation of being touched or movement on the skin when there is no actual stimulus.\n  - Olfactory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of smelling odors that do not exist.\n  - Gustatory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of tasting flavors that are not actually present.\n▸ depressive_episode\n  - Depressed Mood: A persistently sunken mood or feeling of depression.\n  - Tearfulness: A state of frequent crying or inability to hold back tears.\n  - Sadness: A state where a sad or gloomy mood is maintained continuously.\n  - Emptiness: A feeling of inner emptiness or a state of feeling no emotion.\n  - Emotional Numbness: A state where emotions feel dulled or paralyzed.\n  - Loss of Interest: Reduced interest in previously enjoyed hobbies or leisure activities.\n  - Social Withdrawal: Reduced interest or effort in social interactions and maintaining relationships.\n  - Loss of Motivation: Lack of motivation to initiate or complete work or tasks.\n  - Anhedonia: Inability to experience positive emotions such as happiness, satisfaction, love, or pleasure.\n  - Indifference to Achievement: Insensitivity or indifference to personal achievements or success.\n  - Chronic Fatigue: Fatigue that persists for a long period even after adequate rest.\n  - Physical Fatigue: A state of physical exhaustion where the body feels heavy and easily tires even from minor activities.\n  - Mental Fatigue: A state where fatigue accumulates rapidly during the process of thinking or concentrating.\n  - Loss of Energy: Difficulty performing daily activities due to a decline in energy and vitality.\n  - Feelings of Worthlessness: The feeling that one is useless and has no value.\n  - Ruminative Guilt: A thought pattern of obsessively replaying past minor mistakes or flaws and constantly reproaching oneself.\n  - Excessive or Inappropriate Guilt: Feeling excessive responsibility for trivial matters or daily events and believing one deserves punishment.\n  - Delusional Guilt: Guilt reaching a delusional level, disconnected from reality, involving feeling responsible for unrelated unfortunate events.\n  - Recurrent Thoughts of Death: Passive wish to die (e.g., 'I wish I wouldn't wake up') without active intent to kill oneself.\n  - Suicidal Ideation: Recurring thoughts of 'I want to die' or 'I want to kill myself' without a specific plan.\n  - Suicidal Impulse: Strong, sudden urges to jump from a height or run into traffic without a concrete plan.\n  - Specific Plan for Suicide: Conceiving specific scenarios or methods regarding when, where, and how to die.\n  - Suicide Attempt: Actual actions taken to end one's life or preparatory behaviors like hoarding pills or writing a will.\n  Duration: 7 months\n▸ Critical Diagnostic Criteria\n  - Additional: delusions_and_hallucinations occur exclusively during the manic episode\n\nRules:\n- If the patient can specifically answer based on the profile, set matched=true, list related\n  sections/items in matched_sections, and write a short English strategy in answer_strategy\n  on what tone and content the patient should reply with.\n- If the question is not in the profile or can only be inferred, set matched=false and instruct\n  in answer_strategy to respond briefly (e.g., \"I'm not sure\", \"I haven't thought about it\").\n- If patient_turn_index is 1 (patient's first reply), ONLY focus the strategy on the chief complaint\n  (do NOT reveal other symptoms).\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"matched\": true|false, \"matched_sections\": [\"...\"], \"answer_strategy\": \"...\"}\n"

Elevated Mood: A mood that is abnormally high, euphoric, and full of unmotivated happiness and vitality.\n  - Irritable Mood: A state where one gets angry or aggressive even at minor stimuli.\n  - Mood Lability: Rapid shifts in mood, alternating unpredictably between euphoria and irritability.\n  - Increased Energy: Overflowing physical and mental energy, leading to an inability to stay still and a desire to keep moving.\n  - Hyperactivity: Doing far more work than usual or starting multiple activities simultaneously.\n  - Psychomotor Agitation: Inability to tolerate resting or stopping, driven by a constant need to do something.\n  - Responsiveness to Extraneous Stimuli: Attention is immediately drawn to trivial and unimportant stimuli nearby (e.g., noise, movement).\n  - Frequent Task Switching: Unable to finish one task and constantly shifting attention to new things, starting multiple tasks simultaneously.\n  - Inability to Sustain Attention: Unable to focus on any activity for long, with attention constantly being scattered.\n  Duration: 4 months\n▸ delusions_and_hallucinations\n  - Persecutory Delusions: The false belief that one is being harmed or monitored by other people or organizations.\n  - Referential Delusions: The conviction that ordinary events, remarks, or behaviors have specific relevance to oneself.\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Erotomanic Delusions: The false belief that another person is in love with or has affection for them.\n  - Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Auditory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of hearing voices or sounds when there is no actual sound.\n  - Visual Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of seeing people or objects that do not exist.\n  - Tactile Hallucinations: Experiencing the sensation of being touched or movement on the skin when there is no actual stimulus.\n  - Olfactory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of smelling odors that do not exist.\n  - Gustatory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of tasting flavors that are not actually present.\n▸ depressive_episode\n  - Depressed Mood: A persistently sunken mood or feeling of depression.\n  - Tearfulness: A state of frequent crying or inability to hold back tears.\n  - Sadness: A state where a sad or gloomy mood is maintained continuously.\n  - Emptiness: A feeling of inner emptiness or a state of feeling no emotion.\n  - Emotional Numbness: A state where emotions feel dulled or paralyzed.\n  - Loss of Interest: Reduced interest in previously enjoyed hobbies or leisure activities.\n  - Social Withdrawal: Reduced interest or effort in social interactions and maintaining relationships.\n  - Loss of Motivation: Lack of motivation to initiate or complete work or tasks.\n  - Anhedonia: Inability to experience positive emotions such as happiness, satisfaction, love, or pleasure.\n  - Indifference to Achievement: Insensitivity or indifference to personal achievements or success.\n  - Chronic Fatigue: Fatigue that persists for a long period even after adequate rest.\n  - Physical Fatigue: A state of physical exhaustion where the body feels heavy and easily tires even from minor activities.\n  - Mental Fatigue: A state where fatigue accumulates rapidly during the process of thinking or concentrating.\n  - Loss of Energy: Difficulty performing daily activities due to a decline in energy and vitality.\n  - Feelings of Worthlessness: The feeling that one is useless and has no value.\n  - Ruminative Guilt: A thought pattern of obsessively replaying past minor mistakes or flaws and constantly reproaching oneself.\n  - Excessive or Inappropriate Guilt: Feeling excessive responsibility for trivial matters or daily events and believing one deserves punishment.\n  - Delusional Guilt: Guilt reaching a delusional level, disconnected from reality, involving feeling responsible for unrelated unfortunate events.\n  - Recurrent Thoughts of Death: Passive wish to die (e.g., 'I wish I wouldn't wake up') without active intent to kill oneself.\n  - Suicidal Ideation: Recurring thoughts of 'I want to die' or 'I want to kill myself' without a specific plan.\n  - Suicidal Impulse: Strong, sudden urges to jump from a height or run into traffic without a concrete plan.\n  - Specific Plan for Suicide: Conceiving specific scenarios or methods regarding when, where, and how to die.\n  - Suicide Attempt: Actual actions taken to end one's life or preparatory behaviors like hoarding pills or writing a will.\n  Duration: 7 months\n▸ Critical Diagnostic Criteria\n  - Additional: delusions_and_hallucinations occur exclusively during the manic episode\n\n[Behavioral Guidelines]\n- In the very first turn, only talk about your chief complaint. Subtypes: Childlike Silliness, Stupor, Catalepsy, Waxy Flexibility, Mutism, Negativism, Posturing, Mannerism, Stereotypy, Agitation, Grimacing, Echolalia, Echopraxia\n[S023] Negative_Symptoms: A state where emotions, motivation, speech, or social interest that should normally be present are diminished or lost.  Subtypes: Diminished Emotional Expression, Avolition, Alogia, Anhedonia, Asociality\n[S024] Depressed_Mood: A state where a sad, empty, or hopeless mood persists.  Subtypes: Depressed Mood, Tearfulness, Sadness, Emptiness, Emotional Numbness\n[S025] Loss_of_Interest: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.  Subtypes: Loss of Interest, Social Withdrawal, Loss of Motivation, Anhedonia, Indifference to Achievement\n[S026] Weight_Appetite_Change: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.  Subtypes: Significant Weight Loss, Weight Gain, Decrease in Appetite, Increase in Appetite\n[S027] Sleep_Disturbance: A state where there is a significant change in the quantity or quality of sleep.  Subtypes: Initial Insomnia, Middle Insomnia, Early Morning Awakening, Hypersomnia, Nightmare Disorder, Non-Restorative Sleep\n[S028] Psychomotor_Changes: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.  Subtypes: Psychomotor Agitation, Psychomotor Retardation (Motor), Psychomotor Retardation (Speech)\n[S029] Fatigue: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.  Subtypes: Chronic Fatigue, Physical Fatigue, Mental Fatigue, Loss of Energy\n[S030] Worthlessness_Guilt: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick).  Subtypes: Feelings of Worthlessness, Ruminative Guilt, Excessive or Inappropriate Guilt, Delusional Guilt\n[S031] Diminished_Thinking: Diminished ability to think or concentrate, or indecisiveness.  Subtypes: Reduced Concentration, Indecisiveness, Subjective Memory Complaints, Clouded Thinking\n[S032] Death_Suicide: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.  Subtypes: Recurrent Thoughts of Death, Suicidal Ideation, Suicidal Impulse, Specific Plan for Suicide, Suicide Attempt\n[S083] Hopelessness: A feeling of despair or having no hope for the future. Subtypes: Elevated Mood, Irritable Mood, Mood Lability\n[S034] Increased_Activity: Abnormally and persistently increased goal-directed activity or energy.  Subtypes: Increased Energy, Hyperactivity, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S035] Inflated_Self_Esteem: Inflated self-esteem or grandiosity (may range from uncritical self-confidence to delusional proportions).  Subtypes: Inflated Self-Confidence, Overestimation of Abilities, Grandiose Delusions\n[S036] Decreased_Sleep: Decreased need for sleep (e.g., feels rested after only 3 hours of sleep).  Subtypes: Reduced Sleep Duration, Loss of Sleep Need, Indifference to Insomnia\n[S037] Pressured_Speech: More talkative than usual or pressure to keep talking.  Subtypes: Talkativeness, Rapid Speech, Pressured Speech\n[S038] Racing_Thoughts: Flight of ideas or subjective experience that thoughts are racing.  Subtypes: Racing Thoughts, Flooding of Thoughts, Inability to Control Thoughts\n[S039] Manic_Distractibility: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.  Subtypes: Responsiveness to Extraneous Stimuli, Frequent Task Switching, Inability to Sustain Attention\n[S040] Increased_Goal_Activity: Increase in goal-directed activity (at work, school, socially, or sexually) or purposeless psychomotor agitation.  Subtypes: Occupational Hyperactivity, Social Hyperactivity, Hypersexuality, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S041] Painful_Consequence_Behavior: Excessive involvement in activities that have a high potential for painful consequences.  Subtypes: Unrestrained Buying Sprees, Foolish Business Investments, Sexual Indiscretions, Impulsive Risky Behavior\n[S042] Low_Self_Esteem: A persistent tendency to evaluate oneself as incompetent or worthless; chronically low self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Feelings of Worthlessness, Self-Criticism, Reduced Self-Efficacy, Chronic Sense of Inferiority, Lack of Self-Assurance\n[S043] Restrictive_Intake: Persistent restriction of energy intake relative to requirements, leading to significantly low body weight.  Subtypes: Eating Very Small Amounts, Skipping Meals, Exclusion of Specific Food Groups, Strict Calorie Restriction\n[S044] Low_Weight: A body weight that is significantly below the minimum level of health for age, sex, developmental trajectory, and physical health.  Subtypes: Continuous Weight Loss, Insistence on Low Weight\n[S045] Fear_of_Weight_Gain: Intense fear of gaining weight or of becoming fat, even though at a significantly low weight.  Subtypes: Fear of Weight Gain, Fear of Specific Foods, Obsession with Weight Gain\n[S046] Body_Image_Weight_Shape_Disturbance: Undue influence of body weight or shape on self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Distorted Body Image, Self-Worth Dependent on Weight\n[S047] Compensatory_Behavior_NonPurging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors to prevent weight gain without purging (direct removal of food).  Subtypes: Fasting, Excessive Exercise\n[S048] Compensatory_Behavior_Purging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors involving the expulsion of food or fluids to prevent weight gain.  Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities.  Subtypes: Chronic Worry, Generalized Worry, Catastrophizing, Intolerance of Uncertainty\n[S057] Uncontrollable_Worry: The subjective experience of finding it difficult to stop or control the worry.  Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge.  Subtypes: Feeling Keyed Up, Physical Tension, Nervousness\n[S059] Concentration_Difficulty: Difficulty concentrating or mind going blank.  Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort.  Subtypes: Neck and Shoulder Tension, Jaw Tension, Generalized Muscle Aches, Chronic Stiffness\n[S062] Specific_Phobic_Fear: Marked fear or anxiety about a specific object or situation that is out of proportion to the actual danger. Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others. Subtypes: Depressed Mood, Tearfulness, Sadness, Emptiness, Emotional Numbness\n[S025] Loss_of_Interest: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.  Subtypes: Loss of Interest, Social Withdrawal, Loss of Motivation, Anhedonia, Indifference to Achievement\n[S026] Weight_Appetite_Change: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.  Subtypes: Significant Weight Loss, Weight Gain, Decrease in Appetite, Increase in Appetite\n[S027] Sleep_Disturbance: A state where there is a significant change in the quantity or quality of sleep.  Subtypes: Initial Insomnia, Middle Insomnia, Early Morning Awakening, Hypersomnia, Nightmare Disorder, Non-Restorative Sleep\n[S028] Psychomotor_Changes: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.  Subtypes: Psychomotor Agitation, Psychomotor Retardation (Motor), Psychomotor Retardation (Speech)\n[S029] Fatigue: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.  Subtypes: Chronic Fatigue, Physical Fatigue, Mental Fatigue, Loss of Energy\n[S030] Worthlessness_Guilt: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick). Subtypes: Elevated Mood, Irritable Mood, Mood Lability\n[S034] Increased_Activity: Abnormally and persistently increased goal-directed activity or energy. Subtypes: Occupational Hyperactivity, Social Hyperactivity, Hypersexuality, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S041] Painful_Consequence_Behavior: Excessive involvement in activities that have a high potential for painful consequences. Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.
